| 421 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! We left the Bustling Market, and now we're on wide green plains. Whoosh! Can you feel it? The wind is very strong here. Welcome to the Windy Plains! | 422 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! It's a perfect day for kites. The wind is strong, and the sky is blue. Riko is on the hill. Haru wants to know what she's doing. Today's sentence is: What are you doing? Say it with me. What are you doing? Good! And Riko answers, I'm flying a kite. Say it. I'm flying a kite. I'm is I am. I'm flying means I am flying right now. 「なにを しているの？」は What are you doing?。こたえは I'm ～ing. だよ。 Now, phonics. Ride, riding. Hide, hiding. Slide, sliding. Write, writing. Do you remember? When a word ends with e, take the e away and add i-n-g. Excellent! Here's a talking tip. When your friend tells you what they're doing, you can say, That looks fun! Let's say it. That looks fun! Now, let's grab our kites and run up the hill. Ready? Let's fly! | Hi, everyone! It's a perfect day for kites. The wind is strong, and the sky is blue. Riko is on the hill. Haru wants to know what she's doing. Today's sentence is: What are you doing? Say it with me. What are you doing? Good! And Riko answers, I'm flying a kite. Say it. I'm flying a kite. I'm is I am. I'm flying means I am flying right now. 「なにを しているの？」は What are you doing? こたえは I'm ～ing. だよ。 Now, phonics. Ride, riding. Hide, hiding. Slide, sliding. Write, writing. Do you remember? When a word ends with e, take the e away and add i-n-g. Excellent! Here's a talking tip. When your friend tells you what they're doing, you can say, That looks fun! Let's say it. That looks fun! | 423 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today there's a race on the plains. Ready, set, go! Kiko is very fast. Haru is fast, too. When we talk about another person, we say he's or she's. Today's sentence is: Look! She's running fast. Say it with me. Look! She's running fast. Great! She's means she is. He's means he is. ほかの 人が いま している ことは、He's ～ing. / She's ～ing. で いうよ。 Start with Look! Then everyone looks at the same thing. This is how sports reporters talk. They're telling us what's happening now. Now, phonics. Dance, dancing. Race, racing. Chase, chasing. Bounce, bouncing. These words end with c-e or s-e. Take away the e and add i-n-g. Here's a cheering tip. When your friend is running, shout, Go, go, go! You can do it! Let's cheer together. Go, go, go! You can do it! Okay, reporters, get your voices ready. The race is starting! | Hello, everyone! Today there's a race on the plains. Ready, set, go! Kiko is very fast. Haru is fast, too. When we talk about another person, we say he's or she's. | 425 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today Ms. Emma is in the town, and you're on the plains. Let's call her on a video call. On a video call, your friend can't see everything. So tell them what you're doing. Today's sentence is: I'm sitting on a hill. Say it with me. I'm sitting on a hill. Great! You can also say we. We're watching the clouds. We're means we are. And ask back, What are you doing now? ビデオ通話では、I'm ～ing. で いまの ようすを つたえよう。 Now, phonics review. Skate, skating. Dive, diving. These drop the e. Jump, jumping. Play, playing. These just add i-n-g. Look at the last letter! Here's an important tip for video calls. You can talk about what you're doing, but don't say your address or your school's name. じゅうしょや がっこうの なまえは いわないでね。 Okay, hold up your tablets. Let's call Ms. Emma! | Hello, everyone! | 431 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! The children on the plains are playing soccer. They ask us to join them. Let's play! Today we're learning two kinds of sentences. Listen. I play soccer every day. That's a habit. いつも する こと だよ. I'm playing soccer now. That's happening right now. いま している こと だよ。 Say them with me. I play soccer every day. I'm playing soccer now. Great! Every day, usually and on Sundays go with I play. Now and look go with I'm playing. Can you hear the difference? Now, new phonics. Football. Foot and ball. Playground. Play and ground. Homework. Home and work. Weekend. Week and end. Two words become one word. 2つの ことばが くっついて いるよ。 Here's a team tip. When your friend wants to play, say, Come and join us! Let's say it. Come and join us! Okay, lace up your shoes. Let's play some soccer! | Hello, everyone! The children on the plains are playing soccer. They ask us to join them. Let's play! Today we're learning two kinds of sentences. | 432 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! Today is a mini soccer game. Our team plays against the Plains team. In a game, players talk a lot. Today's sentences are: Pass the ball! Nice try! Say them with me. Pass the ball! Nice try! Great! Pass the ball means kick it to me. Nice try means you didn't make it, but it was a good try. おしい！は Nice try! だよ。 When nobody is near you, shout, I'm open! Over here! When your friend makes a good pass, say, Good pass! Now, phonics. Goalkeeper. Goal and keeper. Teammate. Team and mate. Baseball. Base and ball. Basketball. Basket and ball. Long words are easy if you split them. Here's an important tip. Good teammates always say kind words, even when they lose. Say, Nice try! and Good game! Let's say them. Nice try! Good game! Okay, team. Let's go! | Hi, everyone! Today is a mini soccer game. | 433 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! There's a marathon on the plains today. Riko is running in it. It's a long race, and she's getting tired. Let's cheer for her. Today's sentence is: Keep going! You can do it! Say it with me. Keep going! You can do it! Wonderful! Keep going means don't stop, continue. そのまま つづけて、という いみ だよ。 You can also say, Don't give up! And near the end, You're almost there! Cheering words give people power. Use a big voice and a big smile. Now, phonics. Sunglasses. Sun and glasses. Downhill. Down and hill. Raincoat. Rain and coat. Grasshopper. Grass and hopper. A grasshopper is an insect that hops in the grass! Here's a tip. After the race, tell the runner, You did it! I'm proud of you! Let's say it. You did it! Okay, find a good spot by the road. The runners are coming! | Hello, everyone! There's a marathon on the plains today. Riko is running in it. It's a long race, and she's getting tired. Let's cheer for her. Today's sentence is: Keep going! You can do it! Say it with me. Keep going! You can do it! Wonderful! Keep going means don't stop, continue. そのまま つづけて、という いみ だよ。 You can also say, Don't give up! And near the end, You're almost there! Cheering words give people power. Use a big voice and a big smile. Now, phonics. Sunglasses. Sun and glasses. Downhill. Down and hill. Raincoat. Rain and coat. Grasshopper. Grass and hopper. A grasshopper is an insect that hops in the grass! Here's a tip. After the race, tell the runner, You did it! I'm proud of you! Let's say it. You did it! OK, find a good spot by the road. The runners are coming! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

Let's say it. Congratulations! Okay, choose a team. Red or blue? Hold the rope tightly with both hands. Let's pull! | Hi, everyone! It's the tug-of-war contest on the plains. | 436 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we're visiting a painter's studio on the plains. There are so many paintings on the walls! Ms. Emma is pretending to be the painter. Let's talk about the pictures. Today's sentence is: In the picture, a boy is reading. Say it with me. In the picture, a boy is reading. Great! Start with In the picture. Then say who is doing what. You can add There's. There's a dog next to him. え を せつめい するときは、In the picture, からはじめよう。 Now, new phonics. I am becomes I'm. He is becomes he's. She is becomes she's. It is becomes it's. The little mark is called an apostrophe. It shows that a letter is missing. Here's a tip for talking about pictures. You can say what you think, too. I think she's happy. Let's say it. I think she's happy. Okay, let's look at the first painting together! | Hello, everyone! Today we're visiting a painter's studio on the plains. There are so many paintings on the walls! | 437 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! The painter has a very big picture today. It shows a festival on the plains. There are so many people in it! When a picture is big, tell us where to look. | 438 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we're reading the Plains Newspaper. There's a big photo on the front page. Let's talk about it. Today's sentence is: What's happening in this picture? What is, what's. That is, that's. There is, there's. Where is, where's. The i in is is missing, so we write an apostrophe. Here's a talking tip. When you hear a nice idea, say, That's a good idea! | 439 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! Let's play Spot the Difference. Kiko has one picture, and Haru has another. They look the same, but there are five differences. Today's sentence is: In my picture, the cat is sleeping. Say it with me. In my picture, the cat is sleeping. Great! To tell a difference, say, In your picture, it isn't sleeping. It's eating. Isn't means is not. 「わたしの えでは」は In my picture、「あなたの えでは」は In your picture だよ。 For two or more, use aren't. The boys aren't running. Now, phonics. Is not, isn't. Are not, aren't. Do not, don't. | 440 | `teacherScript` | Congratulations, everyone! It's the Picture Talk Show at the studio. Each of you will talk about one picture. A good picture talk has three parts. First, In the picture, and what's happening. Next, people and what they're doing. Last, what you think. Today's sentences are: In the picture, it's raining. People are running. Say them with me. In the picture, it's raining. People are running. Excellent! Then add one more. A girl is holding an umbrella. I think she's kind. 3つの 文と、さいごに I think ～. で じぶんの かんがえ だよ。 Now, phonics review. I am, I'm. They are, they're. Is not, isn't. Let us, let's. Where did the letters go? The apostrophe tells you! Here's a tip for presenting. Point to the picture when you talk. Then everyone can follow you. Start with, Let's look at my picture. Okay, presenters, are you ready? Let's start the show! | Congratulations, everyone! It's the Picture Talk Show at the studio. | 442 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! The rain stopped. Let's go outside. Everything is wet and shiny. Look up! What can you see? Today's sentence is: Look! A rainbow is coming out. Say it with me. Look! A rainbow is coming out. Wonderful! Coming out means appearing. We can also say, The sky is getting brighter. Getting means it's changing little by little. だんだん かわっていく ようすは getting で いうよ。 Can you name the colors of the rainbow? Red, orange, yellow, green, blue and purple. Now, phonics. Sky. Fly. Dry. Why. | 444 | `teacherScript` | Good evening, everyone! It's night on the plains. The campfire is warm, and the sky is very dark. Shh, let's talk quietly. Look up. Today's sentence is: The stars are shining. Say it softly with me. The stars are shining. Beautiful! The moon is rising over the hill. Rising means going up. よるの ようすも ～ing で しずかに いえるよ。 The plains have no city lights, so we can see thousands of stars. Can you find a shooting star? Now, phonics. Sleepy. Starry. These say ee. Shy says eye. Young says yeh. Three sounds of y! Where is the y? At the start, it says yeh. At the end of a long word, ee. At the end of a short word, eye. Here's a tip for bedtime. When you're tired, say, I'm getting sleepy. Good night! Let's whisper it. I'm getting sleepy. Good night! Okay, let's lie down on the blanket and count the stars. | Good evening, everyone! It's night on the plains. The campfire is warm, and the sky is very dark. Shh, let's talk quietly. Look up. Today's sentence is: The stars are shining. Say it softly with me. The stars are shining. Beautiful! The moon is rising over the hill. Rising means going up. よるの ようすも ～ing で しずかに いえるよ。 The plains have no city lights, so we can see thousands of stars. Can you find a shooting star? Now, phonics. Sleepy. Starry. These say ee. Shy says eye. Young says yeh. Three sounds of y! Where is the y? At the start, it says yeh. At the end of a long word, ee. At the end of a short word, eye. Here's a tip for bedtime. When you're tired, say, I'm getting sleepy. Good night! Let's whisper it. I'm getting sleepy. Good night! OK, let's lie down on the blanket and count the stars. | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 445 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! We've had many adventures on the plains. Tonight, let's write a Plains Diary in the tent, and read it to each other. A diary starts with Today. I'm happy because I saw a rainbow. 日記は Today, から はじめて、いまの ようすと きもちを かこう。 Now, phonics review. Yes. Yeh. Happy. Windy. Ee. Fly. Eye. Can you hear all three sounds of y? Fantastic! Here's a tip. When your friend reads their diary, listen and say something nice. That's so nice! Let's say it. That's so nice! And remember, in a diary for sharing, you don't need to write your real name or address. OK, take out your pencils! | A+C | British-leaning praise -> American (same rule as W01-14) ("That's lovely!" -> "That's so nice!", also the aiTask target) |

| 446 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Let's play the Gesture Game. One person acts something out without words. Everyone else guesses what they're doing. To guess, ask a question. Today's sentences are: Are you swimming? No! I'm flying! Say them with me. Are you swimming? No! I'm flying! Great! If the guess is right, say, Yes, I am! If it's wrong, say, No, I'm not. I'm flying! はずれのときは No! I'm ～ing! で こたえよう。 You can ask about another person, too. Is he dancing? Yes, he is! Is she sleeping? No, she isn't! Now, phonics review. Swimming and hopping. Double the letter. Dancing. Drop the e. Flying. Just add i-n-g. Three rules again! Here's a game tip. When someone guesses right, say, You got it! Let's say it. You got it! Okay, who wants to act first? No talking, only gestures! | Hello, everyone! Let's play the Gesture Game. One person acts something out without words. Everyone else guesses what they're doing. To guess, ask a question. Today's sentences are: Are you swimming? No! I'm flying! Say them with me. Are you swimming? No! I'm flying! Great! If the guess is right, say, Yes, I am! Swimming and hopping. Double the letter. Dancing. Drop the e. Flying. Just add i-n-g. Three rules again! Here's a game tip. When someone guesses right, say, You got it! Let's say it. You got it! | 447 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! There's a big kite festival on the plains today. There are so many people! Let's play Who Is It? Describe one person, and your friends find them. Today's sentences are: She's wearing a red hat. She's holding a kite. Say them with me. She's wearing a red hat. She's holding a kite. Wonderful! Wearing is for clothes, hats and glasses. Holding is for things in your hands. wearing は みに つけている もの、holding は てに もっている もの だよ。 You can also say what they're not wearing. He isn't wearing a hat. He's wearing glasses. Then ask, Who is it? Now, phonics review. She is, she's. Is not, isn't. Wear, wearing. Smile, smiling. Contractions and i-n-g words together! Here's a tip. Describe clothes and actions, but be kind. Don't say mean things about people. Okay, look at the crowd. Who is it? | Hi, everyone! There's a big kite festival on the plains today. There are so many people! Let's play Who Is It? He's wearing glasses. Then ask, Who is it? Now, phonics review. She is, she's. Is not, isn't. Wear, wearing. Smile, smiling. Contractions and i-n-g words together! Here's a tip. | 449 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! This is our last day on the Windy Plains. Let's have a goodbye picnic on the hill, and review everything. First, ask your friends, What are you doing? Then answer, I'm eating. I'm flying a kite. Say it with me. What are you doing? Great! Next, report something exciting. Look! A feather is falling from the sky. Remember, every day is for habits, and now is for right now. この ワールドでは、いま している ことを ～ing で いう れんしゅうを したね。 We learned place words, picture talk and cheering words, too. Behind. Between. In the picture. Keep going! Now, phonics review. Windy. Ee. Sky. Eye. Yellow. Yeh. Running. Double the n! Excellent! Here's a tip. At the end of a trip, tell your friends, This was so much fun! Let's say it. This was so much fun! Okay, let's spread the blanket. Wait, what's that in the sky? | Hello, everyone! This is our last day on the Windy Plains. Let's have a goodbye picnic on the hill, and review everything. First, ask your friends, What are you doing? Then answer, I'm eating. I'm flying a kite. Say it with me. What are you doing? Great! Next, report something exciting. Look! A feather is falling from the sky. Remember, every day is for habits, and now is for right now. この ワールドでは、いま している ことを ～ing で いう れんしゅうを したね。 We learned place words, picture talk and cheering words, too. Behind. Between. In the picture. Keep going! Now, phonics review. Windy. Ee. Sky. Eye. Yellow. Yeh. Running. Double the n! Excellent! Here's a tip. At the end of a trip, tell your friends, This was so much fun! Let's say it. | 450 | `teacherScript` | Congratulations, everyone! This is the last lesson of World 15. ワールド15 さいごの レッスンだよ。 First, speed TPR! Look! Stop! Come here! Go! Run! Hop! Swim! Fly! Great! Now, it's the World 15 live show on the plains stage. You are the reporters. Tell the audience what's happening right now. Today's sentence is: We're having a great time on the plains! Say it with me. We're having a great time on the plains! Wonderful! A good live report has three parts. Start with Look! Then say who is doing what. Look! Haru is flying a kite. Riko and Kiko are dancing. Then cheer. Keep going, everyone! Now, the last phonics review. Making. Running. Grasshopper. They're. Happy. Amazing! And look, the wind feather is glowing. It's showing us old stone ruins on the map. Where are we going next? But first, your live show. Speak loudly and smile. You can do it! | Congratulations, everyone! This is the last lesson of World 15. ワールド15 さいごの レッスンだよ。 First, speed challenge! Look! Stop! Come here! Go! Run! Hop! Swim! Fly! Great! Now, it's the World 15 live show on the plains stage. Now, the last phonics review. Making. Running. Grasshopper. They're. Happy. Amazing! And look, the wind feather is glowing. It's showing us old stone ruins on the map. Where are we going next? | 452 | `teacherScript` | Good morning, everyone! We're at the entrance of the ruins. Before we go in, let's talk about yesterday. To ask about yesterday, say, What did you do yesterday? Say it with me. What did you do yesterday? Great! Did is the past form of do. In the answer, we use a past form. I played soccer with my friends. I tried a new food. It was good! きのうの ことは did で きいて、played の ような すぎた かたちで こたえるよ。 Now, phonics. Cry, cried. Try, tried. Carry, carried. Study, studied. When a word ends in a consonant and y, change the y to i and add ed. Let's spell cried together. C, r, i, e, d. Good! Here's a tip. When your friend answers, ask one more question, like, Was it fun? Then it's a real conversation. Okay, let's ask our friends about yesterday! | Good morning, everyone! We're at the entrance of the ruins. Before we go in, let's talk about yesterday. | 453 | `teacherScript` | Hello, explorers! We're walking inside the ruins. It's a little dark, so we have flashlights. Suddenly, Riko says, Stop! There's a big painting on the wall. Later, we can say, We stopped and looked at the wall. Say it with me. We stopped and looked at the wall. Great! You can join two past actions with and. Stopped and looked. Walked and talked. Now, phonics. Stop, stopped. Clap, clapped. Hop, hopped. Drop, dropped. These words have a short vowel and end in one consonant. So we double the last letter and add ed. 2つ かさねる ルールは -ing の ときと おなじだね。 Here's a tip. In a dark place, walk slowly and stay with your group. Don't run! Okay, let's look at the painting together. | 455 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Our first day at the ruins is over. Tonight, let's write a ruins diary in the tent. In a diary, we write about things that already happened, so we use past forms. Today's sentences are: We walked to the ruins. We found a door. Say them with me. We walked to the ruins. We found a door. Great! Walked has ed, but found is a special past form. Find, found. 日記は すぎた ことだから、すぎた かたちで かくよ。 You can also use was and were. It was dark, but we weren't scared. Weren't means were not. Now, phonics review. Walked. Arrived. Clapped. Carried. Remember the four ed rules! Here's a tip. In a diary for sharing, you don't need to write your real name or address. Okay, let's read our diaries to each other! | Hello, everyone! Our first day at the ruins is over. Tonight, let's write a ruins diary in the tent. In a diary, we write about things that already happened, so we use past forms. Today's sentences are: We walked to the ruins. We found a door. Say them with me. We walked to the ruins. We found a door. Great! Walked has ed, but found is a special past form. Find, found. 日記は すぎた ことだから、すぎた かたちで かくよ。 You can also use was and were. It was dark, but we weren't scared. Weren't means were not. Now, phonics review. Walked. Arrived. Clapped. Carried. Remember the four ed rules! Here's a tip. In a diary for sharing, you don't need to write your real name or address. OK, let's read our diaries to each other! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 456 | `teacherScript` | Good morning, everyone! Last night, we all made a campfire together in the plaza. Do you remember? Today's sentence is: We sang a song by the campfire. Say it with me. We sang a song by the campfire. Wonderful! Sang is the past form of sing. Some verbs don't use ed. They change their spelling instead. sing の すぎた かたちは singed では なく、sang だよ。 Now, phonics. Sing, sang. Swim, swam. Drink, drank. Ring, rang. Can you hear it? The i changes to a. Let's say them again quickly. Sang, swam, drank, rang! Great! You can also ask a friend, Did you sing, too? Yes, I did. Here's a tip. A campfire is very hot, so always make one with a grown-up. Never play with fire. Okay, let's talk about our campfire night! | Good morning, everyone! Last night, we all made a campfire together in the plaza. Do you remember? Today's sentence is: We sang a song by the campfire. Say it with me. We sang a song by the campfire. Wonderful! Sang is the past form of sing. Some verbs don't use ed. They change their spelling instead. sing の すぎた かたちは singed では なく、sang だよ。 Now, phonics. Sing, sang. Swim, swam. Drink, drank. Ring, rang. Can you hear it? The i changes to a. Let's say them again quickly. Sang, swam, drank, rang! Great! You can also ask a friend, Did you sing, too? Yes, I did. Here's a tip. A campfire is very hot, so always make one with a grown-up. Never play with fire. OK, let's talk about our campfire night! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 457 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Look, there's an old mailbox near the ruins. And it still works! Last night, Riko wrote a letter. Today's sentence is: I wrote a letter to my grandma. Say it with me. I wrote a letter to my grandma. Great! Wrote is the past form of write. We'll practice words like this soon. A letter starts with Dear. Dear Grandma. And it often ends with Love, and your name. てがみの はじめは Dear ～, だよ。 Now, phonics. Begin, began. Give, gave. Sink, sank. Drink, drank. The i changes to a again. Here's a tip. On an envelope, we write an address. But on the internet or in a chat, never share your address. That's a safety rule. Okay, who will you write to? Let's write a short letter! | Hello, everyone! Look, there's an old mailbox near the ruins. And it still works! Last night, Riko wrote a letter. Today's sentence is: I wrote a letter to my grandma. Say it with me. I wrote a letter to my grandma. Begin, began. Give, gave. Sink, sank. Drink, drank. The i changes to a again. Here's a tip. On an envelope, we write an address. But on the internet or in a chat, never share your address. | 458 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we found a big stone door. There's a riddle on it. I have many words, but I can't talk. You open me, but I'm not a door. What am I? Think hard! Birimo knew the answer. It's a book! Today's sentence is: Birimo knew the answer! Say it with me. Birimo knew the answer! Great! Knew is the past form of know. The k is silent, so we don't say it. know も knew も k は よまないよ。 If you didn't know, say, I didn't know the answer. Now, phonics. Swim, swam. Ring, rang. Sing, sang. Begin, began. The i changes to a. Here's a tip. When a friend gets the answer, say, Good job! And when you don't know, that's okay. Okay, let's make riddles for each other! | Hello, everyone! Today we found a big stone door. There's a riddle on it. I have many words, but I can't talk. You open me, but I'm not a door. What am I? Think hard! Birimo knew the answer. It's a book! Today's sentence is: Birimo knew the answer! Say it with me. Birimo knew the answer! Great! Knew is the past form of know. The k is silent, so we don't say it. know も knew も k は よまないよ。 If you didn't know, say, I didn't know the answer. Now, phonics. Swim, swam. Ring, rang. Sing, sang. Begin, began. The i changes to a. Here's a tip. When a friend gets the answer, say, Good job! And when you don't know, that's okay. OK, let's make riddles for each other! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 460 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! We're taking a break at the rest area of the ruins. Let's have a weekend talk. Today's sentences are: I went to the river. I caught a fish. Now, phonics review. Swam. Sang. Stopped. Tried. Do you remember the rules? Here's a tip. Near a river, always stay with a grown-up, and don't go into deep water. | 461 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! There's a souvenir shop near the gate of the ruins. Souvenirs are things you buy on a trip to remember it. Today's sentence is: I bought a map at the shop. Say it with me. I bought a map at the shop. Great! Bought is the past form of buy. Buy, bought. It looks very different! | 462 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Last night, something funny happened. Haru woke up and saw something white moving in the dark. Was it a ghost? No! It was just a sheet drying in the wind. Today's sentence is: I thought it was a ghost! Say it with me. I thought it was a ghost! Great! Thought is the past form of think. We use I thought when we were wrong about something. かんちがい した ときに つかえるよ。 You can also ask, Were you scared? Yes, I was. Let's practice. Were you scared? Yes, I was. Or, No, I wasn't. Now, phonics. Wake, woke. Break, broke. Speak, spoke. Choose, chose. Again, the past form has o and magic e. Here's a tip. If you feel scared at night, that's okay. Tell a grown-up. Okay, let's tell our funny stories! | Hello, everyone! Last night, something funny happened. Haru woke up and saw something white moving in the dark. Was it a ghost? No! It was just a sheet drying in the wind. Today's sentence is: I thought it was a ghost! Say it with me. I thought it was a ghost! Great! Thought is the past form of think. Wake, woke. Break, broke. Speak, spoke. Choose, chose. Again, the past form has o and magic e. Here's a tip. If you feel scared at night, that's okay. Tell a grown-up. OK, let's tell our funny stories! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 463 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we visit a pot maker's workshop at the ruins. Ms. Emma will play the pot maker. Look at these beautiful old patterns. To ask who taught someone, say, Who taught you? Say it with me. Who taught you? Great! Taught is the past form of teach. To answer, say, My grandpa taught me. Or say it short, My grandpa did. Who ～? の こたえは「～ did.」と みじかく いっても いいよ。 Let's practice. Who taught you? My mom taught me. My mom did. Now, phonics. Know, knew. Grow, grew. Throw, threw. Blow, blew. The past form ends in ew. It sounds like oo, as in moon. Here's a tip. When someone teaches you something, say, Thank you for teaching me! Let's say it. Thank you for teaching me! Okay, let's ask the pot maker! | Hello, everyone! Today we visit a pot maker's workshop at the ruins. Ms. Emma will play the pot maker. Look at these beautiful old patterns. To ask who taught someone, say, Who taught you? Say it with me. Who taught you? Great! Taught is the past form of teach. To answer, say, My grandpa taught me. Or say it short, My grandpa did. Who ～? の こたえは「～ did.」と みじかく いっても いいよ。 Let's practice. Who taught you? My mom taught me. My mom did. Now, phonics. Know, knew. Grow, grew. Throw, threw. Blow, blew. The past form ends in ew. It sounds like oo, as in moon. Here's a tip. When someone teaches you something, say, Thank you for teaching me! Let's say it. Thank you for teaching me! OK, let's ask the pot maker! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 464 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Look up. Can you see the top of the tower? There are one hundred stairs! Let's climb. Today's sentence is: It wasn't easy, but I did it. Say it with me. It wasn't easy, but I did it. Great! Wasn't means was not. You can use this sentence for things that were hard for you. You can also ask, Were you tired? Yes, I was. Or, No, I wasn't. was / were の いいえは wasn't / weren't だよ。 Now, phonics. Fly, flew. Draw, drew. Throw, threw. Grow, grew. The past form ends in ew again. Here's a tip. On high stairs, hold the rail and never push your friends. Go one step at a time. Okay, let's climb carefully and talk about hard things we did! | Hello, everyone! Look up. Can you see the top of the tower? There are one hundred stairs! Let's climb. Today's sentence is: It wasn't easy, but I did it. Say it with me. It wasn't easy, but I did it. Great! Wasn't means was not. You can use this sentence for things that were hard for you. You can also ask, Were you tired? Yes, I was. Or, No, I wasn't. was / were の いいえは wasn't / weren't だよ。 Now, phonics. Fly, flew. Draw, drew. Throw, threw. Grow, grew. The past form ends in ew again. Here's a tip. On high stairs, hold the rail and never push your friends. Go one step at a time. OK, let's climb carefully and talk about hard things we did! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 465 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! What a busy day at the ruins! We climbed the tower, and at the top, we found an old key under a stone. Let's look back on our day. Today's sentence is: We climbed, we found a key, and we laughed. Say it with me. We climbed, we found a key, and we laughed. Great! You can put three past actions in one sentence. Use commas, and put and before the last one. 3つの ことを いう ときは、さいごの まえに and を つけるよ。 Then ask a friend, What was your favorite part? Now, phonics review. Wrote. Froze. Grew. Flew. Which ones have o and magic e? Which ones end in ew? Here's a tip. At the end of the day, share one happy thing with your family. Okay, let's look back on our day! | Hello, everyone! What a busy day at the ruins! We climbed the tower, and at the top, we found an old key under a stone. Let's look back on our day. Today's sentence is: We climbed, we found a key, and we laughed. Say it with me. We climbed, we found a key, and we laughed. Great! You can put three past actions in one sentence. Use commas, and put and before the last one. 3つの ことを いう ときは、さいごの まえに and を つけるよ。 Then ask a friend, What was your favorite part? Now, phonics review. Wrote. Froze. Grew. Flew. Which ones have o and magic e? Which ones end in ew? Here's a tip. At the end of the day, share one happy thing with your family. OK, let's look back on our day! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 466 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we found a big stone with old words on it. It's a story! Many stories start with Once upon a time. These past forms have ought. The g and h are silent. Here's a tip. When you listen to a story, make pictures in your head. Okay, let's read the story on the stone! | Hello, everyone! | 467 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Let's read more of the king's story. Stories happen in order, so we use special words to show the order. First. Then. After that. Today's sentences are: First, he opened the box. Then, he looked inside. Say them with me. First, he opened the box. Then, he looked inside. Great! What did he find? A tiny seed! After that, he planted the seed. じゅんばんを あらわす ことばを つかうと、おはなしが わかりやすく なるよ。 Let's say the order words again. First. Then. After that. To ask about a story, say, What happened next? Now, phonics. Catch, caught. Teach, taught. Buy, bought. Think, thought. Caught and taught have aught. Bought and thought have ought. They sound the same! Here's a tip. Remember the cooking class in World 14? First, then, after that. It's the same! Okay, let's read on! | Hello, everyone! Let's read more of the king's story. Stories happen in order, so we use special words to show the order. First. Then. | 468 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we read the end of the king's story. The seed grew and grew into a big tree. Children came and played under it. | 470 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Tonight is story time in the plaza of the ruins. Each of us will tell a short story. Use these story words. Once upon a time. First. Then. After that. | 471 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we meet a traveler at the ruins. Birimo will play the traveler, Leo. Let's talk about summer vacation. To ask, say, How was your summer? Say it with me. How was your summer? Great! To answer, say, It was great! Or, It was fun! Then add one more sentence about what you did. I went to the mountains. And then ask back, How about you? How about you? と ききかえすと、かいわが つづくよ。 Now, phonics. Today, let's listen to the sounds of ed. Played. The ed sounds like d. Jumped. It sounds like t. Wanted. It sounds like id. Enjoyed. It sounds like d. Here's a tip. When you say jumped, the ed is short and quiet. Don't say jump-ed! Okay, let's share our summer stories! | Hello, everyone! Today we meet a traveler at the ruins. Birimo will play the traveler, Leo. Let's talk about summer vacation. To ask, say, How was your summer? Say it with me. How was your summer? Great! To answer, say, It was great! Or, It was fun! Then add one more sentence about what you did. I went to the mountains. And then ask back, How about you? How about you? と ききかえすと、かいわが つづくよ。 Now, phonics. Today, let's listen to the sounds of ed. Played. The ed sounds like d. Jumped. It sounds like t. Wanted. It sounds like id. Enjoyed. It sounds like d. Here's a tip. When you say jumped, the ed is short and quiet. Don't say jump-ed! OK, let's share our summer stories! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 473 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Good listeners react to stories. Today, let's learn two reactions. For a happy story, say, Sounds fun! Say it with me. Sounds fun! For a bad story, say, Oh no! What happened? Say it with me. Oh no! What happened? Great! たのしい はなしには Sounds fun!、こまった はなしには Oh no! だよ。 Then you can ask, Are you okay now? Yes, I'm fine. Let's practice. I lost my pencil case yesterday. Oh no! What happened? I went to the zoo with my class. Sounds fun! Wonderful reactions! Now, phonics. Listen to the ed sounds. Dropped, t. Cried, d. Shouted, id. Missed, t. Here's a tip. Everyone makes mistakes. When a friend shares a funny mistake, don't laugh at them. Laugh with them. Okay, let's tell our stories and react! | Hello, everyone! | 475 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we're reporters. Let's do a memory interview. To ask about the time, say, When did you learn to ride a bike? Say it with me. When did you learn to ride a bike? Great! Answers can be short. Last year. When I was five. Then ask, How was it? It was hard at first. And then, How was it in the end? It was great! いつ？ どうだった？ と きくと、おもいでの はなしが ひろがるよ。 Let's practice once more. When did you learn to swim? When I was six. How was it? It was fun! Now, phonics review. Asked. Worried. Nodded. Smiled. Do you remember the four ed rules? Here's a tip. In an interview, you don't need to say your real name. Just have fun and listen well. Okay, let's start the interview! | Hello, everyone! Today we're reporters. Let's do a memory interview. To ask about the time, say, When did you learn to ride a bike? Say it with me. When did you learn to ride a bike? Great! Answers can be short. Last year. When I was five. Then ask, How was it? It was hard at first. And then, How was it in the end? It was great! いつ？ どうだった？ と きくと、おもいでの はなしが ひろがるよ。 Let's practice once more. When did you learn to swim? When I was six. How was it? It was fun! Now, phonics review. Asked. Worried. Nodded. Smiled. Do you remember the four ed rules? Here's a tip. In an interview, you don't need to say your real name. Just have fun and listen well. OK, let's start the interview! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 477 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Birimo drew a picture story with four pictures. Let's tell the story together. Picture one. Today's sentences are: Yesterday, Kiko went to the park. She lost her hat. Say them with me. Yesterday, Kiko went to the park. She lost her hat. Great! Lost is the past form of lose. Picture two. The wind blew hard. Picture three. Her hat flew into a tree, and she cried. Picture four. A tall man hurried over and caught it. Finally, she got her hat back. Let's say the ending together. | 479 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! This is our last tour of the Ruins of Memory. Let's look back at everything we did. Ask your friends, What did you do at the ruins? Where did you go first? | 480 | `teacherScript` | Congratulations, everyone! This is the last lesson of World 16. ワールド16 さいごの レッスンだよ。 First, speed TPR! Look! Stop! Come here! Go! Sing! Swim! Write! Fly! Great! Now, it's the World 16 story show in the plaza. Today's sentence is: Once upon a time, four friends found a map. Say it with me. Once upon a time, four friends found a map. Wonderful! A good story uses order words. First. Then. After that. Finally. And it uses past forms. Walked. Found. Opened. Smiled. Now, the last phonics review. Lived. Sang. Flew. Thought. Wrote. Amazing! And look, the old diary has one more page. It's a map of the stars! Where are we going next? But first, your story show. Speak loudly and smile. You can do it! | Congratulations, everyone! This is the last lesson of World 16. ワールド16 さいごの レッスンだよ。 First, speed challenge! Look! Stop! Come here! Go! Sing! Swim! Write! Fly! Great! Now, it's the World 16 story show in the plaza. Today's sentence is: Once upon a time, four friends found a map. Say it with me. Once upon a time, four friends found a map. Wonderful! A good story uses order words. First. Then. After that. Finally. And it uses past forms. Walked. Found. Opened. Smiled. Now, the last phonics review. Lived. Sang. Flew. Thought. Wrote. Amazing! And look, the old diary has one more page. It's a map of the stars! Where are we going next? But first, your story show. Speak loudly and smile. You can do it! | B+C | kids do not know the term "TPR"; the say lines already use "speed challenge" (same as W13-14 L390/L420) |
